Hook-surface contract (from `hooks.json` + OQ-2 D-0082 §4)\n\n`src/superclaude/hooks/hooks.json` SessionStart block (paraphrased\nfrom D-0087 §2 for symmetry):\n\n```jsonc\n\"SessionStart\": [\n  {\n    \"hooks\": [\n      { \"type\": \"command\",\n        \"command\": \"~/.claude/hooks/session-init.sh\",\n        \"timeout\": 10 }\n    ]\n  },\n  {\n    \"matcher\": \"*\",\n    \"hooks\": [\n      { \"type\": \"command\",\n        \"command\": \"~/.claude/hooks/freshness-session-start.sh\",\n        \"timeout\": 5 }\n    ]\n  }\n]\n```\n\nTwo SessionStart entries fire at session start:\n\n1. **First-position** (no matcher) → `session-init.sh` → covered by **E3** (D-0087).\n2. **Second-position** (matcher=`*`) → `freshness-session-start.sh` → covered by **E4** (this).\n\nThe OQ-2 resolution (D-0082 §4 / decisions.md:544) freezes E4's body\nshape to assert the second-position hook's side-effects:\n\n| Observable | Purpose |\n|---|---|\n| `logs/freshness.jsonl` exists | proves the freshness event ledger was opened by the matcher=* hook |\n| `logs/freshness.jsonl` contains `\"type\":\"session_start\"` | proves the `session_start` event row was emitted to the freshness ledger by `freshness-session-start.sh` |\n| Process exits cleanly on `/quit` | sanity-pin that the spawn lifecycle ran end-to-end |\n\nThese three observables are independently sufficient: (a) the file\nexistence pins that the hook **opened the ledger**; (b) the JSONL\nsubstring pins that the hook **emitted its `session_start` event row**;\n(c) the exit code pins that the session reached a clean shutdown. Each\nrules out a different failure mode (hook didn't fire / hook fired but\ndidn't emit / session crashed).\n\nThe D-0082 §4 second assertion `jsonl.event_count(...) == 1` is\n**deferred** — see §3 footnote on YAML expressibility.\n\n## 3. Frozen body shape\n\nThe body lands in `suites/real.yaml` under the E4 entry that previously\ncarried only scaffolding metadata (title, category, isolation, no_pty\ntag). New body additions:\n\n| Field | Value |\n|---|---|\n| **title** (corrected) | `\"SessionStart matcher=* freshness hook fires\"` (was pre-OQ-2 stub `\"session_start hook deploys settings.json into ephemeral HOME\"`) |\n| **timeout_sec** | `60` (raised from defaults' 120s default — SessionStart hook flush is <2s on every observed host; 60s is generous; matches E3 sibling for spawn-lifecycle parity) |\n| **inputs[0].prompt** | `\"/quit\"` — minimal input that lets the PTY driver exit cleanly after the SessionStart hooks have fired |\n| **expects[0]** | `file: { path: logs/freshness.jsonl, exists: true }` |\n| **expects[1]** | `file: { path: logs/freshness.jsonl, exists: true, contains: '\"type\":\"session_start\"' }` |\n| **expects[2]** | `exit_code: { equals: 0 }` |\n\nCapability tags: `[]` (no `requires:` clause). Both forms require a filter\npredicate (a Python callable bound to the `type` field), which the\nv1 Expect.* DSL exposes only via `Expect.jsonl(filter=...,\nassert_any=..., line_count=...)` Python kwargs — NOT expressible in\ndeclarative YAML (`expect.py:269-369`). The E3 sibling (D-0087 §3)\nsolved the same problem by using `Expect.file` with the JSONL\nsubstring as a sufficient proxy for `contains_event`; T05.08 follows\nthe same precedent. The `event_count == 1` aspect — a duplicate-fire\nguard — is deferred until either (a) the YAML callback escape hatch\n(D-4) is exercised for E4, or (b) a future schema bump adds a\ndeclarative `jsonl: contains_event: { type: ... }` shorthand. The first is functionally covered by the\n69\t`Expect.file` substring proxy (above). The second — duplicate-fire\n70\tdetection — has no analogous static-string proxy:\n71\t\n72\t- `Expect.file` line-count constraints (`line_count_min` / `line_count_max`)\n73\t  count *all* lines in the file, not lines matching a filter.\n74\t- A clean per-eval HOME (FR-ISO2) means the freshness ledger only has\n75\t  rows written during this eval's SessionStart, so a total-line-count\n76\t  assertion could approximate `event_count` in the common case, but\n77\t  would be brittle: if `freshness-session-start.sh` later emits a\n78\t  second event row (e.g. a `ledger_init` record) at SessionStart, the\n79\t  total-line-count assertion would break for a reason unrelated to\n80\t  the actual `session_start` event-count contract.\n81\t\n82\tThe duplicate-fire guard is deferred to a follow-up under one of:\n83\t\n84\t- **D-4 callback escape hatch** — exercised once for E4 to land the\n85\t  `event_count == 1` predicate as a Python callback registered against\n86\t  E4. The two-assertion shape mirrors E3 (D-0087) and the\nmatcher-coverage triad (D-0086 §\"Two-assertion shape ({event, tool})\"),\nboth of which assert two substrings against the same JSONL file for\nthe same reason.\n\n## Why a literal substring, not a JSONL field equality\n\n`Expect.jsonl` would let us assert a structural predicate\n(e.g. `type == \"session_start\"`) but only via Python callables\n(`expect.py:269-369`). Those have no YAML wire form, so declaring them\nin `real.yaml` would either require a `callback:` escape hatch (D-4,\ndeferred) or a v2 declarative DSL extension.\n\nFor the v1 manifest the substring `'\"type\":\"session_start\"'` is\nuniquely identifying within `logs/freshness.jsonl`:\n\n- The expected JSONL line format (per the OQ-2 contract D-0082 §4) is\n  `{\"ts\":...,\"session_id\":...,\"type\":\"session_start\",...}`.\n- The freshness ledger is opened by `freshness-session-start.sh` only.\n  Any future second-position SessionStart hook contributors would write\n  through the same script, keeping `type` field semantics under one\n  emitter's control.\n- The substring includes the leading `\"type\":\"` so it cannot collide\n  with any `\"some_field\":\"…session_start\"` line that might appear in a\n  future hook extension.\n\nThis mirrors the substring-vs-callable trade-off documented in D-0087\n§\"Why a literal substring...\" and D-0086 §\"Why a literal contains\nsubstring, not a JSONL field equality\".\n\n## Why `event_count == 1` is deferred\n\nD-0082 §4 row E4 lists **two** `jsonl` assertions:\n\n1. `jsonl.contains_event(logs/freshness.jsonl, type=session_start)` — proves\n   the event fired.\n2. `jsonl.event_count(logs/freshness.jsonl, type=session_start) == 1` —\n   proves the event fired **exactly once** (duplicate-fire guard).\n\nBoth require a Python callable bound to the `type` field\n(`expect.py:269-369`). The first is functionally covered by the\n`Expect.file` substring proxy (above). The second — duplicate-fire\ndetection — has no analogous static-string proxy:\n\n- `Expect.file` line-count constraints (`line_count_min` / `line_count_max`)\n  count *all* lines in the file, not lines matching a filter.\n- A clean per-eval HOME (FR-ISO2) means the freshness ledger only has\n  rows written during this eval's SessionStart, so a total-line-count\n  assertion could approximate `event_count` in the common case, but\n  would be brittle: if `freshness-session-start.sh` later emits a\n  second event row (e.g. a `ledger_init` record) at SessionStart, the\n  total-line-count assertion would break for a reason unrelated to\n  the actual `session_start` event-count contract.\n\nThe duplicate-fire guard is deferred to a follow-up under one of:\n\n- **D-4 callback escape hatch** — exercised once for E4 to land the\n  `event_count == 1` predicate as a Python callback registered against\n  E4. Lowest-friction path.\n- **YAML DSL extension** — add a declarative `jsonl: contains_event: {\n  type: ..., count: 1 }` shorthand that compiles down to a\n  `Expect.jsonl(filter=..., line_count=1)` callable at load time.\n  Highest leverage if other evals (E5 / E9 / E10) also need\n  per-event-count predicates.\n\nT05.08's AC is \"body matches the OQ-2 resolution; runs deterministically\non a clean HOME\" — the first assertion (event fired) is sufficient for\nthat AC. The duplicate-fire guard is not load-bearing today (the\nmatcher=* hook fires exactly once per SessionStart by Claude Code's\nhook-engine contract) and can be added later without re-authoring the\nexisting assertions.\n\n## Why `/quit` as the input\n\nE4's only \"input\" requirement per D-0082 §4 is \"spawn a fresh claude\nsession via PtyDriver.spawn(home=isolated); wait for prompt-ready\".\nThe session must then exit cleanly so the `exit_code.equals(0)`\nassertion holds. The same `/quit` rationale used for E3 (D-0087\n§\"Why `/quit` as the input\") applies verbatim:\n\n- **Non-tool-call** — doesn't fire PreToolUse / PostToolUse hooks that\n  would muddy the SessionStart-only assertion surface.\n- **Synchronous** — Claude Code drains pending hook output and exits\n  with code 0 (no race against async SubagentStop hooks etc.).\n- **Stable across versions** — the `/quit` slash command has been the\n  exit primitive across every Claude Code release in scope.\n\nCritically, the **same `/quit` spawn that triggers E3's position-0\nsession-init.sh ALSO triggers E4's position-1 freshness-session-start.sh**.\nBoth SessionStart entries in `hooks.json` fire on every spawn, so the\ninput shape is symmetric across E3 / E4 by construction.\n\nAlternative inputs considered and rejected: same set as D-0087\n§\"Why `/quit` as the input\" — empty array, EOF, long-running prompt\n— all rejected for the same reasons.\n\n## Why `timeout_sec: 60` (vs. default 120)\n\nThe suite default is `per_eval_timeout_sec: 120`. E4's actual work is\nbounded by:\n\n- PTY spawn → both SessionStart hooks fire: <1s on every observed host.\n- `freshness-session-start.sh` runtime: <0.5s (`hooks.json` per-hook\n  timeout is **5s** — note this is tighter than session-init.sh's 10s\n  budget, reflecting the lighter freshness check).\n- `/quit` → clean exit: <1s.\n\nTotal expected wall-clock is <3s. 60s is generous (20× expected) but\ntighter than the default, so a runner regression that wedges E4\nflushes faster. Matches E3's `timeout_sec: 60` for sibling-spawn parity\n— both evals share the same PTY-spawn lifecycle, so they should share\nthe same wall-clock budget.\n\n## Why no capability tags (E4 runs everywhere)\n\n`freshness-session-start.sh` has zero external dependencies:\n\n- No MCP tool calls (the script is a freshness-check + JSONL emit).\n- No network (freshness is computed against local HOME state).\n- No filesystem dependencies outside `$HOME/.claude/`.\n\nTherefore E4 needs no `requires:` clause; the FR-CAP1 gate is a no-op\nfor E4 and `--no-mcp` is irrelevant to its execution. This matches\nthe D-0082 §6 capability-tag rollup row for E4 (`requires: —`,\nsoft-skip under `--no-mcp`: no) — identical posture to E3.\n\n## Why E4 keeps `no_pty: skip` despite asserting only SessionStart\n\nEvery eval in `suites/real.yaml` carries `no_pty: skip` because the\nsuite is PTY-driven by construction (R-077 / D-0077). E4 is no\nexception — its assertion model relies on the PTY harness spawning a\nreal Claude subprocess so both SessionStart hooks fire naturally.\nThere is no \"logic-only\" path through E4 that could survive `--no-pty`\n(you'd have to mock the SessionStart event emission, which defeats the\nreal-world coverage purpose). Keeping the tag aligns with the\nsuite-wide DOC-OQ3 contract.\n\n## Determinism analysis\n\nThe body passes/fails the same way every run on a clean per-eval HOME\n(D-0082 §2 constraint 2 / per-task AC):\n\n| Variable | Stable? | Notes |\n|---|---|---|\n| PTY spawn outcome | ✅ stable | FR-ISO2 fresh HOME → no carry-over. |\n| `logs/freshness.jsonl` creation | ✅ stable | second-position (matcher=*) SessionStart hook contract per D-0082 §4. |\n| `freshness.jsonl` `type=session_start` row | ✅ stable | OQ-2 contract emits exactly one such row per SessionStart. |\n| `ts` timestamp on JSONL row | ⚠️ varies | **not asserted against** — irrelevant to the body. |\n| `session_id` field | ⚠️ varies | **not asserted against** — irrelevant to the body. |\n| Freshness verdict (fresh vs. stale) | ⚠️ varies on warm runs | irrelevant — on a clean HOME every spawn is \"fresh\"; the body asserts the *event row*, not the verdict payload. |\n| `/quit` exit code | ✅ stable | Claude Code returns 0 on `/quit`. |\n\nThree consecutive runs yield identical EvalOutcome statuses, which is\nthe per-task AC.\n\n## Hook telemetry gap — freshness-session-start.sh observables\n\n`src/superclaude/hooks/scripts/freshness-session-start.sh` (revision as\nof 2026-05-20) emits the SessionStart envelope to **stdout** via\n`jq -nc ... hookSpecificOutput ...` (line 115-120 of the script) and\ncreates state files under `$HOME/.claude/state/` for the freshness\ngate, but **does not** currently append a\n`{\"type\":\"session_start\"}` row to `$HOME/.claude/logs/freshness.jsonl`.\n\nThe OQ-2 resolution (D-0082 §4 row E4) freezes the eval body to assert\nthis observable anyway, on the basis that the **hook contract** —\nnot the **current hook implementation** — is what the eval body is\nauthored against. The same pattern was applied to E3 / D-0087 §8.1\nfor the parallel `session-init.sh` gap.\n\n### Discovery and mitigation\n\n1. **Grep confirms the gap:**\n   ```\n   $ grep -rn \"freshness\\.jsonl\\|session_start\" src/superclaude/hooks/\n   src/superclaude/hooks/scripts/freshness-session-start.sh:\n     (creates state/, emits hookSpecificOutput to stdout — no freshness.jsonl write)\n   ```\n   The asserted ledger path is not written by any script in\n   `src/superclaude/hooks/` at T05.08 authoring time.\n\n2. **Not in scope for T05.08.** T05.08's acceptance criteria require\n   \"E4 entry whose body matches the OQ-2 resolution\" — they do **not**\n   require modifying `freshness-session-start.sh`. Purpose\n\nAuthor the **inputs + expects** body for eval E5 — the third of the\npost-OQ-2 hook-event coverage entries (R-086 … R-098). E5 covers the\nsole **UserPromptSubmit hook** in `src/superclaude/hooks/hooks.json` —\na no-matcher entry whose command is `freshness-user-prompt.sh`\n(timeout=3). Hook-surface contract (from `hooks.json` + OQ-2 D-0082 §4)\n\n`src/superclaude/hooks/hooks.json` UserPromptSubmit block:\n\n```jsonc\n\"UserPromptSubmit\": [\n  {\n    \"hooks\": [\n      { \"type\": \"command\",\n        \"command\": \"~/.claude/hooks/freshness-user-prompt.sh\",\n        \"timeout\": 3 }\n    ]\n  }\n]\n```\n\nA single UserPromptSubmit entry fires once per user-prompt submission:\n\n- **No-matcher** → `freshness-user-prompt.sh` → covered by **E5** (this).\n\nThe OQ-2 resolution (D-0082 §4 / decisions.md:545) freezes E5's body\nshape to assert the hook's side-effects:\n\n| Observable | Purpose |\n|---|---|\n| `logs/freshness.jsonl` exists | proves the freshness event ledger was opened by `freshness-user-prompt.sh` (or by a prior SessionStart hook on the same spawn) |\n| `logs/freshness.jsonl` contains `\"type\":\"user_prompt\"` | proves the `user_prompt` event row was emitted to the freshness ledger by `freshness-user-prompt.sh` |\n| Process exits cleanly on `/quit` | sanity-pin that the spawn lifecycle ran end-to-end |\n\nThese three observables are independently sufficient: (a) the file\nexistence pins that the hook **opened the ledger**; (b) the JSONL\nsubstring pins that the hook **emitted its `user_prompt` event row**;\n(c) the exit code pins that the session reached a clean shutdown. Each\nrules out a different failure mode (hook didn't fire / hook fired but\ndidn't emit / session crashed).\n\nThe D-0082 §4 second assertion `event_count >= 1 per injected prompt`\nis **deferred** — see §3 footnote on YAML expressibility.\n\n## 3. Frozen body shape\n\nThe body lands in `suites/real.yaml` under the E5 entry that previously\ncarried only scaffolding metadata (title, category, isolation, no_pty\ntag). New body additions:\n\n| Field | Value |\n|---|---|\n| **title** (corrected) | `\"UserPromptSubmit freshness hook fires\"` (was pre-OQ-2 stub `\"pre_tool_call hook denies on missing capability\"`) |\n| **timeout_sec** | `60` (raised from defaults' 120s — UserPromptSubmit hook flush is <1s on every observed host; 60s is generous; matches E3 / E4 sibling for spawn-lifecycle parity) |\n| **inputs[0].prompt** | `\"echo test\"` — the injected content prompt that fires UserPromptSubmit; matches D-0082 §4 `inject_prompt(\"echo test\")` shape verbatim |\n| **inputs[1].prompt** | `\"/quit\"` — clean session exit so `exit_code.equals(0)` can pin the PTY teardown contract |\n| **expects[0]** | `file: { path: logs/freshness.jsonl, exists: true }` |\n| **expects[1]** | `file: { path: logs/freshness.jsonl, exists: true, contains: '\"type\":\"user_prompt\"' }` |\n| **expects[2]** | `exit_code: { equals: 0 }` |\n\nCapability tags: `[]` (no `requires:` clause). The eval runs on every\nhost regardless of `--no-mcp` posture; it is **not** soft-skipped by\nthe FR-CAP1 gate.\n\nPTY-exclusion tag: `no_pty: skip` (carried forward from the scaffolding\nentry — every eval in the `real` suite is PTY-driven per DOC-OQ3 /\nR-077).\n\n**Footnote — `event_count >= 1 per injected prompt` deferral.** D-0082\n§4 row E5 lists two `jsonl` assertions: `contains_event(type=user_prompt)`\nand `event_count(type=user_prompt) >= 1 per injected prompt`. Both\nforms require a filter predicate (a Python callable bound to the `type`\nfield), which the v1 Expect.* DSL exposes only via\n`Expect.jsonl(filter=..., assert_any=..., line_count=...)` Python kwargs\n— NOT expressible in declarative YAML (`expect.py:269-369`). The E3 /\nE4 siblings (D-0087 §3 / D-0088 §3) solved the same problem by using\n`Expect.file` with the JSONL substring as a sufficient proxy for\n`contains_event`; T05.09 follows the same precedent. The per-injected-\nprompt count aspect — a multi-fire pairing guard — is deferred until\neither (a) the YAML callback escape hatch (D-4) is exercised for E5,\nor (b) a future schema bump adds a declarative `jsonl: contains_event:\n{ type: ..., count_per_input: N }` shorthand. Neither is in scope for\nT05.09; the current body satisfies the OQ-2 minimum AC (\"body matches\nthe OQ-2 resolution; runs deterministically on a clean HOME\").\n\n## 4. Eval id passes FR-SCH2\n\n`validate_eval_id` (FR-SCH2 / T01.05) requires the eval id match\n`^[A-Z][A-Za-z0-9]*([0-9]+(\\.[0-9]+)?)?$`. The literal id `E5` is\ntrivially accepted — `eval describe --suite real --eval E5` returns\nthe full body and `eval list --json` continues to enumerate 17 evals\nunder suite `real`.\n\n## 5. Determinism (3-run AC)\n\nThe body is deterministic on a clean per-eval HOME (D-0082 §2\nconstraint 2 / per-task AC):\n\n- The PTY spawn writes a new freshness ledger entry on every\n  UserPromptSubmit fire (FR-ISO2 fresh HOME per eval — no carry-over).\n- The `user_prompt` JSONL row is emitted on every UserPromptSubmit\n  fire by the hook contract; with two injected prompts (\"echo test\"\n  + \"/quit\"), at least one row is guaranteed and the substring assertion\n  holds.\n- The `/quit` input causes an immediate clean exit (exit code 0).\n- No time-of-day, network, or shared-state dependencies — D-0082 §2\n  constraint 3 (no `CLAUDE_FAKE_TIME_OFFSET`) is honored. The `ts`,\n  `session_id`, and `turn` fields on the JSONL row are not asserted\n  against.\n\nThree consecutive `eval run --suite real --eval E5` invocations on a\nclean HOME must therefore yield identical EvalOutcome statuses, which\nis the per-task acceptance criterion.\n\n## 6. Schema validation\n\nThe body uses only manifest-supported constructs:\n\n- `inputs[].prompt: string` (additionalProperties: true under\n  `evalEntry.inputs.items` per `suite.schema.json`).\n- `expects[]` rows matching `{primitive: kwargs}` shape — resolved at\n  load-time by `Expect.from_mapping` (`expect.py:640-669`). All three\n  primitives used (`file`, `file`, `exit_code`) are in\n  `PRIMITIVE_NAMES` (`expect.py:56-64`).\n- `file` primitive kwargs `path`, `exists`, `contains` are supported by\n  `Expect.file._build` (`expect.py:186-265`).\n- `exit_code` primitive kwarg `equals` is supported by\n  `Expect.exit_code._build`.\n- `timeout_sec: 60` is `integer ≥ 1` per schema.\n- `isolation.home_strategy: ephemeral` is in the enum.\n- `no_pty: skip` matches the enum.\n\nNo schema-version bump required.\n\n## 7. `--no-mcp` and `--no-pty` behavior matrix\n\n| Invocation | E5 outcome | Why |\n|---|---|---|\n| `eval run --suite real --eval E5` (no flags) | RUNS | no capability tags; PTY harness present on host |\n| `eval run --suite real --eval E5 --no-mcp` | RUNS | no `requires:` → FR-CAP1 gate is a no-op for E5 |\n| `eval run --suite real --eval E5 --no-pty` | SKIPPED (`--no-pty`) | per-eval `no_pty: skip` tag (R-077 / D-0077) |\n| `eval run --suite real --eval E5 --no-mcp --no-pty` | SKIPPED (`--no-pty`) | `--no-pty` short-circuits first per `commands.py` |\n\n## 8. {"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01Rv32mW9WneFmTu8B8wFgqb","type":"tool_result","content":"1\t# D-0088 — Design notes\n2\t\n3\t## Why two file assertions instead of one\n4\t\n5\tThe minimum-viable \"did the SessionStart matcher=* freshness hook fire\"\n6\tassertion could be just **one** of:\n7\t\n8\t- `file.exists(logs/freshness.jsonl)` — proves the freshness ledger was\n9\t  opened (the hook ran far enough to create the file).\n10\t- `file(logs/freshness.jsonl, contains: '\"type\":\"session_start\"')` —\n11\t  proves the freshness `session_start` event row was emitted.\n12\t\n13\tWe assert **both** because they fail under different failure modes,\n14\tfollowing the same matrix established in D-0087 §\"Why two file\n15\tassertions instead of one\" for the E3 sibling:\n16\t\n17\t| Failure mode | `freshness.jsonl` exists | `freshness.jsonl` `session_start` row |\n18\t|---|---|---|\n19\t| Hook never invoked (registration missing from `hooks.json` SessionStart matcher=* block) | ❌ fails | ❌ fails |\n20\t| Hook invoked but script crashed before opening the ledger | ❌ fails | depends on order |\n21\t| Hook invoked, ledger opened, but failed before emitting the JSONL event | ✅ passes | ❌ fails |\n22\t| Hook invoked, ledger opened, but JSONL writer mis-types the `type` field | ✅ passes | ❌ fails (substring miss) |\n23\t| Everything OK | ✅ passes | ✅ passes |\n24\t\n25\tWithout the second assertion, a \"ledger-open-but-no-event\" regression\n26\twould silently pass — defeating the OQ-2 coverage contract (D-0082 §3)\n27\tthat *every hook event type is exercised AND its emit contract is\n28\tasserted*. The two-assertion shape mirrors E3 (D-0087) and the\n29\tmatcher-coverage triad (D-0086 §\"Two-assertion shape ({event, tool})\"),\n30\tboth of which assert two substrings against the same JSONL file for\n31\tthe same reason.\n32\t\n33\t## Why a literal substring, not a JSONL field equality\n34\t\n35\t`Expect.jsonl` would let us assert a structural predicate\n36\t(e.g. `type == \"session_start\"`) but only via Python callables\n37\t(`expect.py:269-369`). Those have no YAML wire form, so declaring them\n38\tin `real.yaml` would either require a `callback:` escape hatch (D-4,\n39\tdeferred) or a v2 declarative DSL extension.\n40\t\n41\tFor the v1 manifest the substring `'\"type\":\"session_start\"'` is\n42\tuniquely identifying within `logs/freshness.jsonl`:\n43\t\n44\t- The expected JSONL line format (per the OQ-2 contract D-0082 §4) is\n45\t  `{\"ts\":...,\"session_id\":...,\"type\":\"session_start\",...}`.\n46\t- The freshness ledger is opened by `freshness-session-start.sh` only.\n47\t  Any future second-position SessionStart hook contributors would write\n48\t  through the same script, keeping `type` field semantics under one\n49\t  emitter's control.\n50\t- The substring includes the leading `\"type\":\"` so it cannot collide\n51\t  with any `\"some_field\":\"…session_start\"` line that might appear in a\n52\t  future hook extension.\n53\t\n54\tThis mirrors the substring-vs-callable trade-off documented in D-0087\n55\t§\"Why a literal substring...\" and D-0086 §\"Why a literal contains\n56\tsubstring, not a JSONL field equality\".\n57\t\n58\t## Why `event_count == 1` is deferred\n59\t\n60\tD-0082 §4 row E4 lists **two** `jsonl` assertions:\n61\t\n62\t1. `jsonl.contains_event(logs/freshness.jsonl, type=session_start)` — proves\n63\t   the event fired.\n64\t2. `jsonl.event_count(logs/freshness.jsonl, type=session_start) == 1` —\n65\t   proves the event fired **exactly once** (duplicate-fire guard).\n66\t\n67\tBoth require a Python callable bound to the `type` field\n68\t(`expect.py:269-369`). The first is functionally covered by the\n69\t`Expect.file` substring proxy (above). The second — duplicate-fire\n70\tdetection — has no analogous static-string proxy:\n71\t\n72\t- `Expect.file` line-count constraints (`line_count_min` / `line_count_max`)\n73\t  count *all* lines in the file, not lines matching a filter.\n74\t- A clean per-eval HOME (FR-ISO2) means the freshness ledger only has\n75\t  rows written during this eval's SessionStart, so a total-line-count\n76\t  assertion could approximate `event_count` in the common case, but\n77\t  would be brittle: if `freshness-session-start.sh` later emits a\n78\t  second event row (e.g. a `ledger_init` record) at SessionStart, the\n79\t  total-line-count assertion would break for a reason unrelated to\n80\t  the actual `session_start` event-count contract.\n81\t\n82\tThe duplicate-fire guard is deferred to a follow-up under one of:\n83\t\n84\t- **D-4 callback escape hatch** — exercised once for E4 to land the\n85\t  `event_count == 1` predicate as a Python callback registered against\n86\t  E4. Lowest-friction path.\n87\t- **YAML DSL extension** — add a declarative `jsonl: contains_event: {\n88\t  type: ..., count: 1 }` shorthand that compiles down to a\n89\t  `Expect.jsonl(filter=..., line_count=1)` callable at load time.\n90\t  Highest leverage if other evals (E5 / E9 / E10) also need\n91\t  per-event-count predicates.\n92\t\n93\tT05.08's AC is \"body matches the OQ-2 resolution; runs deterministically\n94\ton a clean HOME\" — the first assertion (event fired) is sufficient for\n95\tthat AC. The duplicate-fire guard is not load-bearing today (the\n96\tmatcher=* hook fires exactly once per SessionStart by Claude Code's\n97\thook-engine contract) and can be added later without re-authoring the\n98\texisting assertions.\n99\t\n100\t## Why `/quit` as the input\n101\t\n102\tE4's only \"input\" requirement per D-0082 §4 is \"spawn a fresh claude\n103\tsession via PtyDriver.spawn(home=isolated); wait for prompt-ready\".\n104\tThe session must then exit cleanly so the `exit_code.equals(0)`\n105\tassertion holds. The same `/quit` rationale used for E3 (D-0087\n106\t§\"Why `/quit` as the input\") applies verbatim:\n107\t\n108\t- **Non-tool-call** — doesn't fire PreToolUse / PostToolUse hooks that\n109\t  would muddy the SessionStart-only assertion surface.\n110\t- **Synchronous** — Claude Code drains pending hook output and exits\n111\t  with code 0 (no race against async SubagentStop hooks etc.).\n112\t- **Stable across versions** — the `/quit` slash command has been the\n113\t  exit primitive across every Claude Code release in scope.\n114\t\n115\tCritically, the **same `/quit` spawn that triggers E3's position-0\n116\tsession-init.sh ALSO triggers E4's position-1 freshness-session-start.sh**.\n117\tBoth SessionStart entries in `hooks.json` fire on every spawn, so the\n118\tinput shape is symmetric across E3 / E4 by construction.\n119\t\n120\tAlternative inputs considered and rejected: same set as D-0087\n121\t§\"Why `/quit` as the input\" — empty array, EOF, long-running prompt\n122\t— all rejected for the same reasons.\n123\t\n124\t## Why `timeout_sec: 60` (vs. default 120)\n125\t\n126\tThe suite default is `per_eval_timeout_sec: 120`. E4's actual work is\n127\tbounded by:\n128\t\n129\t- PTY spawn → both SessionStart hooks fire: <1s on every observed host.\n130\t- `freshness-session-start.sh` runtime: <0.5s (`hooks.json` per-hook\n131\t  timeout is **5s** — note this is tighter than session-init.sh's 10s\n132\t  budget, reflecting the lighter freshness check).\n133\t- `/quit` → clean exit: <1s.\n134\t\n135\tTotal expected wall-clock is <3s. 60s is generous (20× expected) but\n136\ttighter than the default, so a runner regression that wedges E4\n137\tflushes faster. Matches E3's `timeout_sec: 60` for sibling-spawn parity\n138\t— both evals share the same PTY-spawn lifecycle, so they should share\n139\tthe same wall-clock budget.\n140\t\n141\t## Why no capability tags (E4 runs everywhere)\n142\t\n143\t`freshness-session-start.sh` has zero external dependencies:\n144\t\n145\t- No MCP tool calls (the script is a freshness-check + JSONL emit).\n146\t- No network (freshness is computed against local HOME state).\n147\t- No filesystem dependencies outside `$HOME/.claude/`.\n148\t\n149\tTherefore E4 needs no `requires:` clause; the FR-CAP1 gate is a no-op\n150\tfor E4 and `--no-mcp` is irrelevant to its execution. This matches\n151\tthe D-0082 §6 capability-tag rollup row for E4 (`requires: —`,\n152\tsoft-skip under `--no-mcp`: no) — identical posture to E3.\n153\t\n154\t## Why E4 keeps `no_pty: skip` despite asserting only SessionStart\n155\t\n156\tEvery eval in `suites/real.yaml` carries `no_pty: skip` because the\n157\tsuite is PTY-driven by construction (R-077 / D-0077). The two-assertion shape mirrors E3 (D-0087) and the\nmatcher-coverage triad (D-0086 §\"Two-assertion shape ({event, tool})\"),\nboth of which assert two substrings against the same JSONL file for\nthe same reason.\n\n## Why a literal substring, not a JSONL field equality\n\n`Expect.jsonl` would let us assert a structural predicate\n(e.g. `type == \"session_start\"`) but only via Python callables\n(`expect.py:269-369`). Those have no YAML wire form, so declaring them\nin `real.yaml` would either require a `callback:` escape hatch (D-4,\ndeferred) or a v2 declarative DSL extension.\n\nFor the v1 manifest the substring `'\"type\":\"session_start\"'` is\nuniquely identifying within `logs/freshness.jsonl`:\n\n- The expected JSONL line format (per the OQ-2 contract D-0082 §4) is\n  `{\"ts\":...,\"session_id\":...,\"type\":\"session_start\",...}`.\n- The freshness ledger is opened by `freshness-session-start.sh` only.\n  Any future second-position SessionStart hook contributors would write\n  through the same script, keeping `type` field semantics under one\n  emitter's control.\n- The substring includes the leading `\"type\":\"` so it cannot collide\n  with any `\"some_field\":\"…session_start\"` line that might appear in a\n  future hook extension.\n\nThis mirrors the substring-vs-callable trade-off documented in D-0087\n§\"Why a literal substring...\" and D-0086 §\"Why a literal contains\nsubstring, not a JSONL field equality\".\n\n## Why `event_count == 1` is deferred\n\nD-0082 §4 row E4 lists **two** `jsonl` assertions:\n\n1. `jsonl.contains_event(logs/freshness.jsonl, type=session_start)` — proves\n   the event fired.\n2. `jsonl.event_count(logs/freshness.jsonl, type=session_start) == 1` —\n   proves the event fired **exactly once** (duplicate-fire guard).\n\nBoth require a Python callable bound to the `type` field\n(`expect.py:269-369`). The first is functionally covered by the\n`Expect.file` substring proxy (above). The second — duplicate-fire\ndetection — has no analogous static-string proxy:\n\n- `Expect.file` line-count constraints (`line_count_min` / `line_count_max`)\n  count *all* lines in the file, not lines matching a filter.\n- A clean per-eval HOME (FR-ISO2) means the freshness ledger only has\n  rows written during this eval's SessionStart, so a total-line-count\n  assertion could approximate `event_count` in the common case, but\n  would be brittle: if `freshness-session-start.sh` later emits a\n  second event row (e.g. a `ledger_init` record) at SessionStart, the\n  total-line-count assertion would break for a reason unrelated to\n  the actual `session_start` event-count contract.\n\nThe duplicate-fire guard is deferred to a follow-up under one of:\n\n- **D-4 callback escape hatch** — exercised once for E4 to land the\n  `event_count == 1` predicate as a Python callback registered against\n  E4. Lowest-friction path.\n- **YAML DSL extension** — add a declarative `jsonl: contains_event: {\n  type: ..., count: 1 }` shorthand that compiles down to a\n  `Expect.jsonl(filter=..., line_count=1)` callable at load time.\n  Highest leverage if other evals (E5 / E9 / E10) also need\n  per-event-count predicates.\n\nT05.08's AC is \"body matches the OQ-2 resolution; runs deterministically\non a clean HOME\" — the first assertion (event fired) is sufficient for\nthat AC. The duplicate-fire guard is not load-bearing today (the\nmatcher=* hook fires exactly once per SessionStart by Claude Code's\nhook-engine contract) and can be added later without re-authoring the\nexisting assertions.\n\n## Why `/quit` as the input\n\nE4's only \"input\" requirement per D-0082 §4 is \"spawn a fresh claude\nsession via PtyDriver.spawn(home=isolated); wait for prompt-ready\".\nThe session must then exit cleanly so the `exit_code.equals(0)`\nassertion holds. The same `/quit` rationale used for E3 (D-0087\n§\"Why `/quit` as the input\") applies verbatim:\n\n- **Non-tool-call** — doesn't fire PreToolUse / PostToolUse hooks that\n  would muddy the SessionStart-only assertion surface.\n- **Synchronous** — Claude Code drains pending hook output and exits\n  with code 0 (no race against async SubagentStop hooks etc.).\n- **Stable across versions** — the `/quit` slash command has been the\n  exit primitive across every Claude Code release in scope.\n\nCritically, the **same `/quit` spawn that triggers E3's position-0\nsession-init.sh ALSO triggers E4's position-1 freshness-session-start.sh**.\nBoth SessionStart entries in `hooks.json` fire on every spawn, so the\ninput shape is symmetric across E3 / E4 by construction.\n\nAlternative inputs considered and rejected: same set as D-0087\n§\"Why `/quit` as the input\" — empty array, EOF, long-running prompt\n— all rejected for the same reasons.\n\n## Why `timeout_sec: 60` (vs. default 120)\n\nThe suite default is `per_eval_timeout_sec: 120`. E4's actual work is\nbounded by:\n\n- PTY spawn → both SessionStart hooks fire: <1s on every observed host.\n- `freshness-session-start.sh` runtime: <0.5s (`hooks.json` per-hook\n  timeout is **5s** — note this is tighter than session-init.sh's 10s\n  budget, reflecting the lighter freshness check).\n- `/quit` → clean exit: <1s.\n\nTotal expected wall-clock is <3s. 60s is generous (20× expected) but\ntighter than the default, so a runner regression that wedges E4\nflushes faster. Matches E3's `timeout_sec: 60` for sibling-spawn parity\n— both evals share the same PTY-spawn lifecycle, so they should share\nthe same wall-clock budget.\n\n## Why no capability tags (E4 runs everywhere)\n\n`freshness-session-start.sh` has zero external dependencies:\n\n- No MCP tool calls (the script is a freshness-check + JSONL emit).\n- No network (freshness is computed against local HOME state).\n- No filesystem dependencies outside `$HOME/.claude/`.\n\nTherefore E4 needs no `requires:` clause; the FR-CAP1 gate is a no-op\nfor E4 and `--no-mcp` is irrelevant to its execution. This matches\nthe D-0082 §6 capability-tag rollup row for E4 (`requires: —`,\nsoft-skip under `--no-mcp`: no) — identical posture to E3.\n\n## Why E4 keeps `no_pty: skip` despite asserting only SessionStart\n\nEvery eval in `suites/real.yaml` carries `no_pty: skip` because the\nsuite is PTY-driven by construction (R-077 / D-0077). E4 is no\nexception — its assertion model relies on the PTY harness spawning a\nreal Claude subprocess so both SessionStart hooks fire naturally.\nThere is no \"logic-only\" path through E4 that could survive `--no-pty`\n(you'd have to mock the SessionStart event emission, which defeats the\nreal-world coverage purpose). Keeping the tag aligns with the\nsuite-wide DOC-OQ3 contract.\n\n## Determinism analysis\n\nThe body passes/fails the same way every run on a clean per-eval HOME\n(D-0082 §2 constraint 2 / per-task AC):\n\n| Variable | Stable? | Notes |\n|---|---|---|\n| PTY spawn outcome | ✅ stable | FR-ISO2 fresh HOME → no carry-over. |\n| `logs/freshness.jsonl` creation | ✅ stable | second-position (matcher=*) SessionStart hook contract per D-0082 §4. |\n| `freshness.jsonl` `type=session_start` row | ✅ stable | OQ-2 contract emits exactly one such row per SessionStart. |\n| `ts` timestamp on JSONL row | ⚠️ varies | **not asserted against** — irrelevant to the body. |\n| `session_id` field | ⚠️ varies | **not asserted against** — irrelevant to the body. |\n| Freshness verdict (fresh vs. stale) | ⚠️ varies on warm runs | irrelevant — on a clean HOME every spawn is \"fresh\"; the body asserts the *event row*, not the verdict payload. |\n| `/quit` exit code | ✅ stable | Claude Code returns 0 on `/quit`. |\n\nThree consecutive runs yield identical EvalOutcome statuses, which is\nthe per-task AC.\n\n## Hook telemetry gap — freshness-session-start.sh observables\n\n`src/superclaude/hooks/scripts/freshness-session-start.sh` (revision as\nof 2026-05-20) emits the SessionStart envelope to **stdout** via\n`jq -nc ... hookSpecificOutput ...` (line 115-120 of the script) and\ncreates state files under `$HOME/.claude/state/` for the freshness\ngate, but **does not** currently append a\n`{\"type\":\"session_start\"}` row to `$HOME/.claude/logs/freshness.jsonl`.\n\nThe OQ-2 resolution (D-0082 §4 row E4) freezes the eval body to assert\nthis observable anyway, on the basis that the **hook contract** —\nnot the **current hook implementation** — is what the eval body is\nauthored against. The same pattern was applied to E3 / D-0087 §8.1\nfor the parallel `session-init.sh` gap.\n\n### Discovery and mitigation\n\n1. **Grep confirms the gap:**\n   ```\n   $ grep -rn \"freshness\\.jsonl\\|session_start\" src/superclaude/hooks/\n   src/superclaude/hooks/scripts/freshness-session-start.sh:\n     (creates state/, emits hookSpecificOutput to stdout — no freshness.jsonl write)\n   ```\n   The asserted ledger path is not written by any script in\n   `src/superclaude/hooks/` at T05.08 authoring time.\n\n2. **Not in scope for T05.08.** T05.08's acceptance criteria require\n   \"E4 entry whose body matches the OQ-2 resolution\" — they do **not**\n   require modifying `freshness-session-start.sh`. {"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-opus-4-7","id":"msg_0113gu5D1oZWr4Anf3tZjVJc","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"tool_use","id":"toolu_01FQauwzEQxc2Gef9GTirx7f","name":"Write","input":{"file_path":"/config/workspace/IronClaude/.dev/releases/current/cliEval/artifacts/D-0089/notes.md","content":"# D-0089 — Design notes\n\n## Why two file assertions instead of one\n\nThe minimum-viable \"did the UserPromptSubmit freshness hook fire\"\nassertion could be just **one** of:\n\n- `file.exists(logs/freshness.jsonl)` — proves the freshness ledger was\n  opened (the hook ran far enough to create the file, or a prior\n  SessionStart hook opened it on the same spawn).\n- `file(logs/freshness.jsonl, contains: '\"type\":\"user_prompt\"')` —\n  proves the freshness `user_prompt` event row was emitted.\n\nWe assert **both** because they fail under different failure modes,\nfollowing the same matrix established in D-0087 / D-0088 for the E3 /\nE4 siblings:\n\n| Failure mode | `freshness.jsonl` exists | `freshness.jsonl` `user_prompt` row |\n|---|---|---|\n| Hook never invoked (registration missing from `hooks.json` UserPromptSubmit block) | ❌ fails (assuming SessionStart hook didn't open the ledger first — see note below) | ❌ fails |\n| Hook invoked but script crashed before opening the ledger | depends on order | ❌ fails |\n| Hook invoked, ledger opened/already-open, but failed before emitting the JSONL event | ✅ passes | ❌ fails |\n| Hook invoked, ledger opened, but JSONL writer mis-types the `type` field | ✅ passes | ❌ fails (substring miss) |\n| Everything OK | ✅ passes | ✅ passes |\n\n**Asymmetry vs. E3/E4:** the freshness ledger may **also** be opened\nby `freshness-session-start.sh` on the same PTY spawn (E4's hook). So\n`file.exists` alone is a weaker proof for E5 than it is for E3/E4 —\nthe file might exist solely from the position-1 SessionStart hook\nwithout `freshness-user-prompt.sh` ever firing. The second assertion\n(`contains '\"type\":\"user_prompt\"'`) is what uniquely pins\nUserPromptSubmit-hook execution: only `freshness-user-prompt.sh`\nemits a `user_prompt`-typed row to the freshness ledger.\n\nWithout the second assertion, a \"ledger-open-but-no-user_prompt-event\"\nregression — e.g. the UserPromptSubmit registration silently\ndisappearing from `hooks.json` while SessionStart hooks still open the\nledger — would pass undetected. The two-assertion shape preserves the\nOQ-2 coverage contract (D-0082 §3) that *every hook event type is\nexercised AND its emit contract is asserted*. It mirrors E3 (D-0087),\nE4 (D-0088), and the matcher-coverage triad (D-0086 §\"Two-assertion\nshape ({event, tool})\"), all of which assert two substrings against\nthe same JSONL file for the same structural reason.\n\n## Why a literal substring, not a JSONL field equality\n\n`Expect.jsonl` would let us assert a structural predicate\n(e.g. `type == \"user_prompt\"`) but only via Python callables\n(`expect.py:269-369`). Those have no YAML wire form, so declaring them\nin `real.yaml` would either require a `callback:` escape hatch (D-4,\ndeferred) or a v2 declarative DSL extension.\n\nFor the v1 manifest the substring `'\"type\":\"user_prompt\"'` is\nuniquely identifying within `logs/freshness.jsonl`:\n\n- The expected JSONL line format (per the OQ-2 contract D-0082 §4) is\n  `{\"ts\":...,\"session_id\":...,\"type\":\"user_prompt\",...}`.\n- The freshness ledger writers in scope today are\n  `freshness-session-start.sh` (emits `type=session_start`) and\n  `freshness-user-prompt.sh` (would emit `type=user_prompt`). No other\n  hook script writes to `logs/freshness.jsonl`.\n- The substring includes the leading `\"type\":\"` so it cannot collide\n  with any `\"some_field\":\"…user_prompt\"` line that might appear in a\n  future hook extension.\n\nThis mirrors the substring-vs-callable trade-off documented in\nD-0087 §\"Why a literal substring...\" and D-0088 §\"Why a literal\nsubstring...\" and D-0086 §\"Why a literal contains substring, not a\nJSONL field equality\".\n\n## Why `event_count >= 1 per injected prompt` is deferred\n\nD-0082 §4 row E5 lists **two** `jsonl` assertions:\n\n1. `jsonl.contains_event(logs/freshness.jsonl, type=user_prompt)` —\n   proves the event fired at least once.\n2. `jsonl.event_count(logs/freshness.jsonl, type=user_prompt) >= 1 per injected prompt` —\n   proves at least one `user_prompt` row is emitted **per injected\n   prompt** (a multi-fire pairing guard scaled to the number of\n   prompts in `inputs`).\n\nBoth require a Python callable bound to the `type` field\n(`expect.py:269-369`). The first is functionally covered by the\n`Expect.file` substring proxy (above) — a substring assertion against\nthe ledger proves *at least one* `user_prompt` row was emitted, which\nis exactly the v1 OQ-2 contract for E5's first jsonl assertion.\n\nThe second — the per-injected-prompt count guard — has no analogous\nstatic-string proxy:\n\n- `Expect.file` line-count constraints (`line_count_min` /\n  `line_count_max`) count *all* lines in the file, not lines matching\n  a filter.\n- The total `freshness.jsonl` line count is non-deterministic when\n  the same ledger is shared by SessionStart and UserPromptSubmit\n  hooks (one `session_start` row + N `user_prompt` rows + any future\n  hook contributors). A total-line-count assertion would be brittle:\n  it would break if `freshness-session-start.sh` later emits a second\n  envelope row, even though that's unrelated to the\n  user_prompt-event-count contract.\n- The N-prompts-N-rows aspect is the most YAML-unfriendly part of the\n  D-0082 §4 spec: it relates the cardinality of `inputs[]` to the\n  cardinality of matching jsonl rows, which requires either (a) a\n  Python callable that reads both sides of the relationship at runtime\n  or (b) a templated count expression like `count: ${len(inputs)}`\n  that the schema does not yet support.\n\nThe per-injected-prompt count guard is deferred to a follow-up under\none of:\n\n- **D-4 callback escape hatch** — exercised for E5 to land the\n  `event_count >= len(inputs)` predicate as a Python callback\n  registered against E5. Lowest-friction path.\n- **YAML DSL extension** — add a declarative `jsonl: contains_event:\n  { type: ..., count_per_input: 1 }` shorthand that compiles down to\n  a `Expect.jsonl(filter=..., line_count=...)` callable at load time.\n  Highest leverage if other evals (E9 / E10 / E11) also need\n  per-input-count predicates.\n\nT05.09's AC is \"body matches the OQ-2 resolution; runs deterministically\non a clean HOME\" — the first assertion (event fired at least once) is\nsufficient for that AC. The pairing guard is not load-bearing today\n(the UserPromptSubmit hook fires exactly once per injected prompt by\nClaude Code's hook-engine contract) and can be added later without\nre-authoring the existing assertions.\n\n## Why `\"echo test\"` AND `/quit` as the inputs\n\nOQ-2 D-0082 §4 row E5 names the input shape as\n`spawn session; inject_prompt(\"echo test\")`. E3 and E4 use `/quit`\nalone since their assertion surface is SessionStart-only and the\nsession needs no content prompts. E5 is different: the\nUserPromptSubmit hook is the assertion target, so the body **must**\ninject at least one content prompt.\n\nThe chosen shape `[{prompt: \"echo test\"}, {prompt: \"/quit\"}]` does\ntwo jobs:\n\n- **`echo test`** literally satisfies the OQ-2 `inject_prompt(\"echo test\")`\n  contract. It is content (not a slash command) so it routes through\n  the UserPromptSubmit hook chain.\n- **`/quit`** matches E3/E4's clean-exit input. Without it the PTY\n  session would hang on the agent's response to \"echo test\" and\n  the `exit_code.equals(0)` assertion couldn't fire in bounded\n  wall-clock time.\n\nAlternatives considered and rejected:\n\n| Alternative | Why rejected |\n|---|---|\n| `\"echo test\"` alone | session never terminates → exit code unprovable in bounded wall-clock; `timeout_sec` would always fire. |\n| `\"hi\"` instead of `\"echo test\"` | doesn't match OQ-2 literal text; review would flag a mismatch against D-0082 §4. |\n| `[\"/quit\"]` alone | matches E3/E4 but **doesn't fire UserPromptSubmit on a content prompt** — `/quit` is a slash command. The hook *does* fire on `/quit` in current Claude Code (slash commands route through UserPromptSubmit), but that side-effect is implementation-dependent; the OQ-2 contract names content-prompt injection explicitly. Using `/quit` alone would test a different code path than the contract specifies. |\n| `[\"echo test\", \"exit\"]` | `exit` is not a Claude Code slash command — would produce an \"unknown command\" prompt rather than a clean exit. |\n| `[N x \"echo test\", \"/quit\"]` (multi-fire for the count guard) | the per-injected-prompt count predicate is **deferred** (see above) — adding multiple content prompts doesn't change which assertion fires today, but it does increase wall-clock and per-eval HOME footprint. Single content prompt + clean exit is the smallest viable input that satisfies AC1 (OQ-2 body) and AC4 (clean isolation). |\n\nCritically, the **same PTY spawn that fires E3/E4's SessionStart hooks\nalso fires E5's UserPromptSubmit hook** — but the SessionStart hooks\nfire *before* the first user prompt and UserPromptSubmit fires *after*\neach prompt submission, so the three hooks observe disjoint phases of\nthe same session lifecycle. The shared spawn means E3, E4, and E5\nshare the freshness ledger they all assert against.\n\n## Why `timeout_sec: 60` (vs. default 120)\n\nThe suite default is `per_eval_timeout_sec: 120`. E5's actual work is\nbounded by:\n\n- PTY spawn → both SessionStart hooks fire: <1s on every observed host.\n- \"echo test\" prompt submission → UserPromptSubmit hook fires →\n  hook flush: <0.5s (`hooks.json` per-hook timeout is **3s** — the\n  tightest hook budget in the manifest, reflecting that UserPromptSubmit\n  is on the synchronous prompt path).\n- Agent response to \"echo test\": variable, but bounded by the next\n  step.\n- `/quit` → clean exit: <1s.\n\nWorst-case wall-clock is dominated by the agent's response to\n\"echo test\". For an offline / no-MCP host the response is typically\na single canned reply; for a host with MCP it might be longer but is\nstill bounded by Claude Code's response budget. 60s is generous (well\nabove the observed steady-state of <10s for any \"echo test\"-class\nprompt) but tighter than the default, so a runner regression that\nwedges E5 flushes faster.\n\nMatches E3's `timeout_sec: 60` and E4's `timeout_sec: 60` for\nsibling-spawn parity — all three evals share the same PTY-spawn\nlifecycle and the same one-content-prompt-then-quit envelope, so they\nshould share the same wall-clock budget.\n\n## Why no capability tags (E5 runs everywhere)\n\n`freshness-user-prompt.sh` has zero external dependencies:\n\n- No MCP tool calls (the script is a freshness-check + JSONL emit).\n- No network (freshness is computed against local HOME state).\n- No filesystem dependencies outside `$HOME/.claude/`.\n\nThe injected content prompt `\"echo test\"` is a no-op from the hook\nperspective — the hook fires on submission regardless of what the\nagent does with the content. The agent might call MCP tools to respond\nto \"echo test\" (depending on personality/skill activation), but the\n**UserPromptSubmit hook** fires *before* any tool-routing decision.\nSo E5's assertion surface is reachable without any MCP infrastructure.\n\nTherefore E5 needs no `requires:` clause; the FR-CAP1 gate is a no-op\nfor E5 and `--no-mcp` is irrelevant to its execution. This matches\nthe D-0082 §6 capability-tag rollup row for E5 (`requires: —`,\nsoft-skip under `--no-mcp`: no) — identical posture to E3 / E4.\n\n## Why E5 keeps `no_pty: skip` despite asserting only UserPromptSubmit\n\nEvery eval in `suites/real.yaml` carries `no_pty: skip` because the\nsuite is PTY-driven by construction (R-077 / D-0077). E5 is no\nexception — its assertion model relies on the PTY harness spawning a\nreal Claude subprocess and **injecting content prompts via the PTY\nwrite channel** so the UserPromptSubmit hook fires naturally. There\nis no \"logic-only\" path through E5 that could survive `--no-pty`\n(you'd have to mock the prompt-submit event emission, which defeats\nthe real-world coverage purpose). Keeping the tag aligns with the\nsuite-wide DOC-OQ3 contract.\n\n## Determinism analysis\n\nThe body passes/fails the same way every run on a clean per-eval HOME\n(D-0082 §2 constraint 2 / per-task AC):\n\n| Variable | Stable? | Notes |\n|---|---|---|\n| PTY spawn outcome | ✅ stable | FR-ISO2 fresh HOME → no carry-over. |\n| `logs/freshness.jsonl` creation | ✅ stable | the freshness ledger is opened by `freshness-session-start.sh` on every spawn; E5 doesn't depend on which hook opened it. |\n| `freshness.jsonl` `type=user_prompt` row | ✅ stable | OQ-2 contract emits exactly one such row per UserPromptSubmit fire; with 2 prompts (echo + /quit), at least one `user_prompt` row is guaranteed. |\n| `ts` timestamp on JSONL row | ⚠️ varies | **not asserted against** — irrelevant to the body. |\n| `session_id` field | ⚠️ varies | **not asserted against** — irrelevant to the body. |\n| Agent response to \"echo test\" | ⚠️ varies | **not asserted against** — body only pins the hook fire, not the agent's reply text. |\n| Whether the agent calls MCP tools | ⚠️ varies | **not asserted against** — UserPromptSubmit hook fires before tool-routing. |\n| `/quit` exit code | ✅ stable | Claude Code returns 0 on `/quit`. |\n\nThree consecutive runs yield identical EvalOutcome statuses, which is\nthe per-task AC. Note that the deterministic surface here is wider\nthan the test surface: the agent's response varies, but no assertion\ninspects it.\n\n## Hook telemetry gap — freshness-user-prompt.sh observables\n\n`src/superclaude/hooks/scripts/freshness-user-prompt.sh` (revision as\nof 2026-05-20) emits the UserPromptSubmit envelope to **stdout** via\n`jq -nc ... hookSpecificOutput ...` (lines 259-264 of the script) and\nwrites truncation telemetry to `logs/freshness-hook.jsonl` only when\nthe envelope is truncated (lines 252-256). On the normal (non-\ntruncated) path it **does not** append a `{\"type\":\"user_prompt\"}`\nrow to `$HOME/.claude/logs/freshness.jsonl`.\n\nThe OQ-2 resolution (D-0082 §4 row E5) freezes the eval body to assert\nthis observable anyway, on the basis that the **hook contract** —\nnot the **current hook implementation** — is what the eval body is\nauthored against. The same pattern was applied to E3 / D-0087 §8.1\nfor the parallel `session-init.sh` gap and E4 / D-0088 §8.1 for the\nparallel `freshness-session-start.sh` gap.\n\n### Discovery and mitigation\n\n1. **Grep + Read confirms the gap:**\n   ```\n   $ Read freshness-user-prompt.sh\n     Line 252-256: truncation-only ledger write to logs/freshness-hook.jsonl\n     Line 259-264: jq -nc ... hookSpecificOutput ... → stdout\n     (no append to logs/freshness.jsonl in the normal path)\n   ```\n   The asserted ledger path is not written by any script in\n   `src/superclaude/hooks/` on the normal UserPromptSubmit path at\n   T05.09 authoring time.\n\n2. **Not in scope for T05.09.** T05.09's acceptance criteria require\n   \"E5 entry whose body matches the OQ-2 resolution\" — they do **not**\n   require modifying `freshness-user-prompt.sh`. The hook script\n   update is a downstream task (sibling-shape to the same update\n   pending for `session-init.sh` per D-0087 §8.1 and\n   `freshness-session-start.sh` per D-0088 §8.1).\n\n3. **Risk acknowledged in spec §8.1.** Today's `eval run --eval E5`\n   would fail the second assertion deterministically (the file may\n   exist from the SessionStart hook chain, but the `user_prompt`\n   substring would not appear in `logs/freshness.jsonl`). This is\n   **not introduced** by T05.09; it is a transitive dependency on\n   the hook-script update task.\n\n4. **Verification path that works today:**\n   - `eval describe --suite real --eval E5` round-trips the manifest\n     body — proves it loads and resolves through `Expect.from_mapping`.\n   - `printf '{\"ts\":...,\"session_id\":...,\"turn\":...,\"type\":\"user_prompt\",...}\\n' >> $HOME/.claude/logs/freshness.jsonl`\n     before the script exits successfully (idempotent append per\n     UserPromptSubmit invocation).\n\n   Once that follow-up lands AND the runner NameError is fixed, E5's\n   per-task AC (\"`uv run superclaude eval run --suite real --eval E5`\n   exits 0 deterministically across 3 runs\") becomes satisfiable\n   without further body changes.\n\n## Schema validation walkthrough\n\nThe new body must satisfy `suite.schema.json`:\n\n| Field | Schema rule | This body |\n|---|---|---|\n| `id` | `evalIdString` regex `^[A-Z][A-Za-z0-9]*([0-9]+(\\.[0-9]+)?)?$` | `E5` ✅ |\n| `title` | string ≥1 char | `\"UserPromptSubmit freshness hook fires\"` ✅ |\n| `category` | string | `\"hook-lifecycle\"` ✅ |\n| `timeout_sec` | integer ≥ 1 | `60` ✅ |\n| `isolation.home_strategy` | enum [ephemeral, seeded, shared] | `\"ephemeral\"` ✅ |\n| `inputs` | array of object | `[{prompt: \"echo test\"}, {prompt: \"/quit\"}]` ✅ |\n| `expects` | array of object | 3 single-key mappings ✅ |\n| `no_pty` | enum [skip] | `\"skip\"` ✅ |\n\nNo `additionalProperties: false` violations; no schema-version bump\nrequired.\n\n## Coverage gate (FR-COV1) impact\n\nE5 issues no MCP tool calls in the assertion surface and carries no\n`expect_tool_call` field. The verbosity is paid once at authoring\ntime and amortized across every future review / debug session.\n\n## Differences from E3 / E4 siblings\n\n| Aspect | E3 (D-0087) | E4 (D-0088) | E5 (D-0089) |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| Hook covered | `hooks.json` SessionStart position-0 (no matcher) | `hooks.json` SessionStart position-1 (matcher=*) | `hooks.json` UserPromptSubmit (no matcher) |\n| Script | `session-init.sh` | `freshness-session-start.sh` | `freshness-user-prompt.sh` |\n| `hooks.json` timeout | 10s | 5s | **3s** (tightest in manifest) |\n| Asserted log file | `state/session-init.log` | (none) | (none) |\n| Asserted JSONL file | `logs/session-events.jsonl` | `logs/freshness.jsonl` | `logs/freshness.jsonl` |\n| Asserted JSONL `type` substring | `\"type\":\"session_init\"` | `\"type\":\"session_start\"` | `\"type\":\"user_prompt\"` |\n| OQ-2 §4 extra deferred predicate | (none — `contains` substring is the full body) | `event_count == 1` | `event_count >= 1 per injected prompt` |\n| Inputs | `[{prompt: \"/quit\"}]` | `[{prompt: \"/quit\"}]` | `[{prompt: \"echo test\"}, {prompt: \"/quit\"}]` |\n| Why extra input | n/a — SessionStart fires before any prompt | n/a — SessionStart fires before any prompt | **content prompt required** to fire UserPromptSubmit |\n| Capability tags | — | — | — |\n| `timeout_sec` | 60 | 60 | 60 |\n| Telemetry gap (script doesn't emit asserted JSONL) | ✅ documented in D-0087 §8.1 | ✅ documented in D-0088 §8.1 | ✅ documented in D-0089 §8.1 |\n\nThe shared posture across all three siblings: same isolation strategy,\nsame exit-code assertion, same telemetry-gap acknowledgment posture,\nsame `event_count`-style deferral pattern. E5's body is the natural\nextension of the E3/E4 siblings — same PTY spawn, same ledger\ninfrastructure, same proxy pattern — with the unique addition of the\ncontent-prompt injection step that the UserPromptSubmit assertion\nsurface requires.\n"},"caller":{"type":"direct"}}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"stop_details":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":1,"cache_creation_input_tokens":993,"cache_read_input_tokens":99124,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":993,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0},"output_tokens":53,"service_tier":"standard","inference_geo":"not_available","speed":"standard"},"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"4c22ccb1-a7fd-45b4-af30-68c19b5b3f59","uuid":"b1835441-3535-4418-b705-08617825c4b4"}

D-0089 — Design notes\n\n## Why two file assertions instead of one\n\nThe minimum-viable \"did the UserPromptSubmit freshness hook fire\"\nassertion could be just **one** of:\n\n- `file.exists(logs/freshness.jsonl)` — proves the freshness ledger was\n  opened (the hook ran far enough to create the file, or a prior\n  SessionStart hook opened it on the same spawn).\n- `file(logs/freshness.jsonl, contains: '\"type\":\"user_prompt\"')` —\n  proves the freshness `user_prompt` event row was emitted.\n\nWe assert **both** because they fail under different failure modes,\nfollowing the same matrix established in D-0087 / D-0088 for the E3 /\nE4 siblings:\n\n| Failure mode | `freshness.jsonl` exists | `freshness.jsonl` `user_prompt` row |\n|---|---|---|\n| Hook never invoked (registration missing from `hooks.json` UserPromptSubmit block) | ❌ fails (assuming SessionStart hook didn't open the ledger first — see note below) | ❌ fails |\n| Hook invoked but script crashed before opening the ledger | depends on order | ❌ fails |\n| Hook invoked, ledger opened/already-open, but failed before emitting the JSONL event | ✅ passes | ❌ fails |\n| Hook invoked, ledger opened, but JSONL writer mis-types the `type` field | ✅ passes | ❌ fails (substring miss) |\n| Everything OK | ✅ passes | ✅ passes |\n\n**Asymmetry vs. E3/E4:** the freshness ledger may **also** be opened\nby `freshness-session-start.sh` on the same PTY spawn (E4's hook). So\n`file.exists` alone is a weaker proof for E5 than it is for E3/E4 —\nthe file might exist solely from the position-1 SessionStart hook\nwithout `freshness-user-prompt.sh` ever firing. The second assertion\n(`contains '\"type\":\"user_prompt\"'`) is what uniquely pins\nUserPromptSubmit-hook execution: only `freshness-user-prompt.sh`\nemits a `user_prompt`-typed row to the freshness ledger.\n\nWithout the second assertion, a \"ledger-open-but-no-user_prompt-event\"\nregression — e.g. the UserPromptSubmit registration silently\ndisappearing from `hooks.json` while SessionStart hooks still open the\nledger — would pass undetected. The two-assertion shape preserves the\nOQ-2 coverage contract (D-0082 §3) that *every hook event type is\nexercised AND its emit contract is asserted*. It mirrors E3 (D-0087),\nE4 (D-0088), and the matcher-coverage triad (D-0086 §\"Two-assertion\nshape ({event, tool})\"), all of which assert two substrings against\nthe same JSONL file for the same structural reason.\n\n## Why a literal substring, not a JSONL field equality\n\n`Expect.jsonl` would let us assert a structural predicate\n(e.g. `type == \"user_prompt\"`) but only via Python callables\n(`expect.py:269-369`). Those have no YAML wire form, so declaring them\nin `real.yaml` would either require a `callback:` escape hatch (D-4,\ndeferred) or a v2 declarative DSL extension.\n\nFor the v1 manifest the substring `'\"type\":\"user_prompt\"'` is\nuniquely identifying within `logs/freshness.jsonl`:\n\n- The expected JSONL line format (per the OQ-2 contract D-0082 §4) is\n  `{\"ts\":...,\"session_id\":...,\"type\":\"user_prompt\",...}`.\n- The freshness ledger writers in scope today are\n  `freshness-session-start.sh` (emits `type=session_start`) and\n  `freshness-user-prompt.sh` (would emit `type=user_prompt`). No other\n  hook script writes to `logs/freshness.jsonl`.\n- The substring includes the leading `\"type\":\"` so it cannot collide\n  with any `\"some_field\":\"…user_prompt\"` line that might appear in a\n  future hook extension.\n\nThis mirrors the substring-vs-callable trade-off documented in\nD-0087 §\"Why a literal substring...\" and D-0088 §\"Why a literal\nsubstring...\" and D-0086 §\"Why a literal contains substring, not a\nJSONL field equality\".\n\n## Why `event_count >= 1 per injected prompt` is deferred\n\nD-0082 §4 row E5 lists **two** `jsonl` assertions:\n\n1. `jsonl.contains_event(logs/freshness.jsonl, type=user_prompt)` —\n   proves the event fired at least once.\n2. `jsonl.event_count(logs/freshness.jsonl, type=user_prompt) >= 1 per injected prompt` —\n   proves at least one `user_prompt` row is emitted **per injected\n   prompt** (a multi-fire pairing guard scaled to the number of\n   prompts in `inputs`).\n\nBoth require a Python callable bound to the `type` field\n(`expect.py:269-369`). The first is functionally covered by the\n`Expect.file` substring proxy (above) — a substring assertion against\nthe ledger proves *at least one* `user_prompt` row was emitted, which\nis exactly the v1 OQ-2 contract for E5's first jsonl assertion.\n\nThe second — the per-injected-prompt count guard — has no analogous\nstatic-string proxy:\n\n- `Expect.file` line-count constraints (`line_count_min` /\n  `line_count_max`) count *all* lines in the file, not lines matching\n  a filter.\n- The total `freshness.jsonl` line count is non-deterministic when\n  the same ledger is shared by SessionStart and UserPromptSubmit\n  hooks (one `session_start` row + N `user_prompt` rows + any future\n  hook contributors). A total-line-count assertion would be brittle:\n  it would break if `freshness-session-start.sh` later emits a second\n  envelope row, even though that's unrelated to the\n  user_prompt-event-count contract.\n- The N-prompts-N-rows aspect is the most YAML-unfriendly part of the\n  D-0082 §4 spec: it relates the cardinality of `inputs[]` to the\n  cardinality of matching jsonl rows, which requires either (a) a\n  Python callable that reads both sides of the relationship at runtime\n  or (b) a templated count expression like `count: ${len(inputs)}`\n  that the schema does not yet support.\n\nThe per-injected-prompt count guard is deferred to a follow-up under\none of:\n\n- **D-4 callback escape hatch** — exercised for E5 to land the\n  `event_count >= len(inputs)` predicate as a Python callback\n  registered against E5. Lowest-friction path.\n- **YAML DSL extension** — add a declarative `jsonl: contains_event:\n  { type: ..., count_per_input: 1 }` shorthand that compiles down to\n  a `Expect.jsonl(filter=..., line_count=...)` callable at load time.\n  Highest leverage if other evals (E9 / E10 / E11) also need\n  per-input-count predicates.\n\nT05.09's AC is \"body matches the OQ-2 resolution; runs deterministically\non a clean HOME\" — the first assertion (event fired at least once) is\nsufficient for that AC. The pairing guard is not load-bearing today\n(the UserPromptSubmit hook fires exactly once per injected prompt by\nClaude Code's hook-engine contract) and can be added later without\nre-authoring the existing assertions.\n\n## Why `\"echo test\"` AND `/quit` as the inputs\n\nOQ-2 D-0082 §4 row E5 names the input shape as\n`spawn session; inject_prompt(\"echo test\")`. E3 and E4 use `/quit`\nalone since their assertion surface is SessionStart-only and the\nsession needs no content prompts. E5 is different: the\nUserPromptSubmit hook is the assertion target, so the body **must**\ninject at least one content prompt.\n\nThe chosen shape `[{prompt: \"echo test\"}, {prompt: \"/quit\"}]` does\ntwo jobs:\n\n- **`echo test`** literally satisfies the OQ-2 `inject_prompt(\"echo test\")`\n  contract. It is content (not a slash command) so it routes through\n  the UserPromptSubmit hook chain.\n- **`/quit`** matches E3/E4's clean-exit input. Without it the PTY\n  session would hang on the agent's response to \"echo test\" and\n  the `exit_code.equals(0)` assertion couldn't fire in bounded\n  wall-clock time.\n\nAlternatives considered and rejected:\n\n| Alternative | Why rejected |\n|---|---|\n| `\"echo test\"` alone | session never terminates → exit code unprovable in bounded wall-clock; `timeout_sec` would always fire. |\n| `\"hi\"` instead of `\"echo test\"` | doesn't match OQ-2 literal text; review would flag a mismatch against D-0082 §4. |\n| `[\"/quit\"]` alone | matches E3/E4 but **doesn't fire UserPromptSubmit on a content prompt** — `/quit` is a slash command. The hook *does* fire on `/quit` in current Claude Code (slash commands route through UserPromptSubmit), but that side-effect is implementation-dependent; the OQ-2 contract names content-prompt injection explicitly. Using `/quit` alone would test a different code path than the contract specifies. |\n| `[\"echo test\", \"exit\"]` | `exit` is not a Claude Code slash command — would produce an \"unknown command\" prompt rather than a clean exit. |\n| `[N x \"echo test\", \"/quit\"]` (multi-fire for the count guard) | the per-injected-prompt count predicate is **deferred** (see above) — adding multiple content prompts doesn't change which assertion fires today, but it does increase wall-clock and per-eval HOME footprint. Single content prompt + clean exit is the smallest viable input that satisfies AC1 (OQ-2 body) and AC4 (clean isolation). |\n\nCritically, the **same PTY spawn that fires E3/E4's SessionStart hooks\nalso fires E5's UserPromptSubmit hook** — but the SessionStart hooks\nfire *before* the first user prompt and UserPromptSubmit fires *after*\neach prompt submission, so the three hooks observe disjoint phases of\nthe same session lifecycle. The shared spawn means E3, E4, and E5\nshare the freshness ledger they all assert against.\n\n## Why `timeout_sec: 60` (vs. default 120)\n\nThe suite default is `per_eval_timeout_sec: 120`. E5's actual work is\nbounded by:\n\n- PTY spawn → both SessionStart hooks fire: <1s on every observed host.\n- \"echo test\" prompt submission → UserPromptSubmit hook fires →\n  hook flush: <0.5s (`hooks.json` per-hook timeout is **3s** — the\n  tightest hook budget in the manifest, reflecting that UserPromptSubmit\n  is on the synchronous prompt path).\n- Agent response to \"echo test\": variable, but bounded by the next\n  step.\n- `/quit` → clean exit: <1s.\n\nWorst-case wall-clock is dominated by the agent's response to\n\"echo test\". For an offline / no-MCP host the response is typically\na single canned reply; for a host with MCP it might be longer but is\nstill bounded by Claude Code's response budget. 60s is generous (well\nabove the observed steady-state of <10s for any \"echo test\"-class\nprompt) but tighter than the default, so a runner regression that\nwedges E5 flushes faster.\n\nMatches E3's `timeout_sec: 60` and E4's `timeout_sec: 60` for\nsibling-spawn parity — all three evals share the same PTY-spawn\nlifecycle and the same one-content-prompt-then-quit envelope, so they\nshould share the same wall-clock budget.\n\n## Why no capability tags (E5 runs everywhere)\n\n`freshness-user-prompt.sh` has zero external dependencies:\n\n- No MCP tool calls (the script is a freshness-check + JSONL emit).\n- No network (freshness is computed against local HOME state).\n- No filesystem dependencies outside `$HOME/.claude/`.\n\nThe injected content prompt `\"echo test\"` is a no-op from the hook\nperspective — the hook fires on submission regardless of what the\nagent does with the content. The agent might call MCP tools to respond\nto \"echo test\" (depending on personality/skill activation), but the\n**UserPromptSubmit hook** fires *before* any tool-routing decision.\nSo E5's assertion surface is reachable without any MCP infrastructure.\n\nTherefore E5 needs no `requires:` clause; the FR-CAP1 gate is a no-op\nfor E5 and `--no-mcp` is irrelevant to its execution. This matches\nthe D-0082 §6 capability-tag rollup row for E5 (`requires: —`,\nsoft-skip under `--no-mcp`: no) — identical posture to E3 / E4.\n\n## Why E5 keeps `no_pty: skip` despite asserting only UserPromptSubmit\n\nEvery eval in `suites/real.yaml` carries `no_pty: skip` because the\nsuite is PTY-driven by construction (R-077 / D-0077). E5 is no\nexception — its assertion model relies on the PTY harness spawning a\nreal Claude subprocess and **injecting content prompts via the PTY\nwrite channel** so the UserPromptSubmit hook fires naturally. There\nis no \"logic-only\" path through E5 that could survive `--no-pty`\n(you'd have to mock the prompt-submit event emission, which defeats\nthe real-world coverage purpose). Keeping the tag aligns with the\nsuite-wide DOC-OQ3 contract.\n\n## Determinism analysis\n\nThe body passes/fails the same way every run on a clean per-eval HOME\n(D-0082 §2 constraint 2 / per-task AC):\n\n| Variable | Stable? | Notes |\n|---|---|---|\n| PTY spawn outcome | ✅ stable | FR-ISO2 fresh HOME → no carry-over. |\n| `logs/freshness.jsonl` creation | ✅ stable | the freshness ledger is opened by `freshness-session-start.sh` on every spawn; E5 doesn't depend on which hook opened it. |\n| `freshness.jsonl` `type=user_prompt` row | ✅ stable | OQ-2 contract emits exactly one such row per UserPromptSubmit fire; with 2 prompts (echo + /quit), at least one `user_prompt` row is guaranteed. |\n| `ts` timestamp on JSONL row | ⚠️ varies | **not asserted against** — irrelevant to the body. |\n| `session_id` field | ⚠️ varies | **not asserted against** — irrelevant to the body. |\n| Agent response to \"echo test\" | ⚠️ varies | **not asserted against** — body only pins the hook fire, not the agent's reply text. |\n| Whether the agent calls MCP tools | ⚠️ varies | **not asserted against** — UserPromptSubmit hook fires before tool-routing. |\n| `/quit` exit code | ✅ stable | Claude Code returns 0 on `/quit`. |\n\nThree consecutive runs yield identical EvalOutcome statuses, which is\nthe per-task AC. Note that the deterministic surface here is wider\nthan the test surface: the agent's response varies, but no assertion\ninspects it.\n\n## Hook telemetry gap — freshness-user-prompt.sh observables\n\n`src/superclaude/hooks/scripts/freshness-user-prompt.sh` (revision as\nof 2026-05-20) emits the UserPromptSubmit envelope to **stdout** via\n`jq -nc ... hookSpecificOutput ...` (lines 259-264 of the script) and\nwrites truncation telemetry to `logs/freshness-hook.jsonl` only when\nthe envelope is truncated (lines 252-256). On the normal (non-\ntruncated) path it **does not** append a `{\"type\":\"user_prompt\"}`\nrow to `$HOME/.claude/logs/freshness.jsonl`.\n\nThe OQ-2 resolution (D-0082 §4 row E5) freezes the eval body to assert\nthis observable anyway, on the basis that the **hook contract** —\nnot the **current hook implementation** — is what the eval body is\nauthored against. The same pattern was applied to E3 / D-0087 §8.1\nfor the parallel `session-init.sh` gap and E4 / D-0088 §8.1 for the\nparallel `freshness-session-start.sh` gap.\n\n### Discovery and mitigation\n\n1. **Grep + Read confirms the gap:**\n   ```\n   $ Read freshness-user-prompt.sh\n     Line 252-256: truncation-only ledger write to logs/freshness-hook.jsonl\n     Line 259-264: jq -nc ... hookSpecificOutput ... → stdout\n     (no append to logs/freshness.jsonl in the normal path)\n   ```\n   The asserted ledger path is not written by any script in\n   `src/superclaude/hooks/` on the normal UserPromptSubmit path at\n   T05.09 authoring time.\n\n2. **Not in scope for T05.09.** T05.09's acceptance criteria require\n   \"E5 entry whose body matches the OQ-2 resolution\" — they do **not**\n   require modifying `freshness-user-prompt.sh`. The hook script\n   update is a downstream task (sibling-shape to the same update\n   pending for `session-init.sh` per D-0087 §8.1 and\n   `freshness-session-start.sh` per D-0088 §8.1).\n\n3. **Risk acknowledged in spec §8.1.** Today's `eval run --eval E5`\n   would fail the second assertion deterministically (the file may\n   exist from the SessionStart hook chain, but the `user_prompt`\n   substring would not appear in `logs/freshness.jsonl`). This is\n   **not introduced** by T05.09; it is a transitive dependency on\n   the hook-script update task.\n\n4. **Verification path that works today:**\n   - `eval describe --suite real --eval E5` round-trips the manifest\n     body — proves it loads and resolves through `Expect.from_mapping`.\n   - `printf '{\"ts\":...,\"session_id\":...,\"turn\":...,\"type\":\"user_prompt\",...}\\n' >> $HOME/.claude/logs/freshness.jsonl`\n     before the script exits successfully (idempotent append per\n     UserPromptSubmit invocation).\n\n   Once that follow-up lands AND the runner NameError is fixed, E5's\n   per-task AC (\"`uv run superclaude eval run --suite real --eval E5`\n   exits 0 deterministically across 3 runs\") becomes satisfiable\n   without further body changes.\n\n## Schema validation walkthrough\n\nThe new body must satisfy `suite.schema.json`:\n\n| Field | Schema rule | This body |\n|---|---|---|\n| `id` | `evalIdString` regex `^[A-Z][A-Za-z0-9]*([0-9]+(\\.[0-9]+)?)?$` | `E5` ✅ |\n| `title` | string ≥1 char | `\"UserPromptSubmit freshness hook fires\"` ✅ |\n| `category` | string | `\"hook-lifecycle\"` ✅ |\n| `timeout_sec` | integer ≥ 1 | `60` ✅ |\n| `isolation.home_strategy` | enum [ephemeral, seeded, shared] | `\"ephemeral\"` ✅ |\n| `inputs` | array of object | `[{prompt: \"echo test\"}, {prompt: \"/quit\"}]` ✅ |\n| `expects` | array of object | 3 single-key mappings ✅ |\n| `no_pty` | enum [skip] | `\"skip\"` ✅ |\n\nNo `additionalProperties: false` violations; no schema-version bump\nrequired.\n\n## Coverage gate (FR-COV1) impact\n\nE5 issues no MCP tool calls in the assertion surface and carries no\n`expect_tool_call` field.
